| R38 | gitleaks `generic-api-key` (keyword + entropy) in the composer or the redactor; trufflehog's AWS `SecretPat` `[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40}` or any generic 40-hex/base64 rule; driving the warning row from `HEADER_PATTERN`; Enter as yes, a default-yes, a timeout or an "always send" option; redacting the human turn before the provider request; a `paste-cache/` on disk or chip bodies in `ui.json`/history; writing `ui.json` per keystroke; an opt-in history default (12 §11's reading); a `.jevcodeignore`/gitignore-style denylist in v1; OSC 52 read or automatic OSC 52 write; attaching secret files silently under `--allow-secret-mention`; sending on non-TTY stdin when a hit is present | 47 hits in 5.1 MB of prose (~9 FP/MB) even at entropy ≥ 3.5, and gitleaks ships it only with a large stopword list and path allowlists; indistinguishable from git SHA-1s and integrity hashes and trufflehog reports it only after live verification; 9/9 header hits in repo docs were false positives; Enter is the key the user just pressed and remembered approvals are refused for the same reason as 11 §4j; `y` would silently send `[REDACTED:composer#1]` to the generator; bodies on disk for 30 days is the leak class being prevented; a per-keystroke `ui.json` is a keystroke log; opt-in protects only users who know the flag; Gemini needs a restart to reload `.geminiignore` and the candidate cache already follows the git listing; reads prompt or are denied and writes are a clipboard-injection vector; every mention still asks `y/N`; a script piping a key must fail loudly | 16 §2.1–2.4, §3.2, §4.1–4.6, REJECT; 12 §9, §11; 07 §2.5 |
| R39 | Letting Ink's `ErrorOverview` render (the stack in the frame); modal/floating error dialogs or multi-row banners; sub-second countdown ticks or progress bars for retries; `ESC c` or `ESC[2J` in `fatalExit`/crash restore; printing the fatal message before unmount or while raw mode is on, or `process.stderr.write` immediately followed by `process.exit`; exiting 0 and printing `complete` after `state.json` failed to write; one transcript line per failed write; treating 401/403 as a retryable or step-level failure | 42 rows → `clearTerminal` (`ESC[2J ESC[3J`), scrollback erased, raw mode dropped, engine headless (measured); violates the `rows − 2` budget — blocking panes reuse the `Confirm` rows; the spinner already costs 8 fps and a 1 Hz row meets the ≤ 4/s reduced-motion twin; zero-clear rule, and the terminal was already restored by signal-exit + libuv in the measured path; measured `RAW_AT_FATAL=true` and Node drops pending async stdio writes on `process.exit`; the run is not resumable (`--resume` exits 3); 202 identical lines in 12 steps; three steps burned in 71 ms — it is configuration (exit 2) and belongs in onboarding | 17 §1.1–1.5, §3.2, §4.5, §4.7, §4.8, REJECT R1–R8 |
| R40 | Probing the network at launch or on a timer for an offline banner; logging to stdout/stderr while Ink is mounted, logging raw keystrokes, bodies or prompts, or an async-only log buffer without a synchronous flush for `warn`+; sending reports over the network or bundling `jev.jsonl` request bodies by default; auto-retrying spend-cap 429/402; parsing natural-language "try again in N s" hints in v1; making `/exit` return the last run's exit code by default | Zero network before the first frame is a hard constraint — derive state from failures only; `patchConsole: false` frames corrupt, keys-never-in-logs, Codex #2248 lost buffered lines on fast exit; no network dependency for support and workspace text lives in request bodies; Anthropic: "keeps failing until access resumes"; Codex #4161 shows the provider-phrase zoo while `Retry-After`/`retry-after-ms` headers are already honoured; leaving a session is not a failure and scripts have `--json run:end.exitCode` | 17 §2.1, §2.2, §3.1, §4.4, §4.9, §4.10, REJECT R9–R15 |
| R41 | Keeping "rejected by all twelve files"; treating "VS Code 1.109 default true" as the v1.x kitty-handshake trigger; citing "VS Code 1.93" for OSC 52; listing `ghp_`/`sk-` filters as future work; assuming a Node 22 LTS CI job can run `npm publish` with trusted publishing unmodified; publishing with the current `package.json`; shipping the §1 layout as a simultaneous sum (live 2 + pane 12 + review 16 + queue 3 + composer 6 + status 1); reading `process.stdout.columns` in composer/rule code; building tmux from source on this machine to "confirm locally"; modelling the ~6 s notification as a fixed delay; trusting DEC 2026 inside tmux 3.7/3.7a | Three files never discuss it; the 1.109 default was `false` behind an experiment — use "≥ 1.110" and detect at runtime; wrong by two releases and can-i-use-terminal's version field is unreliable; already implemented and tested in `src/core/redact.ts`, the real gap is AWS/PEM/JWT; bundled npm is 10.9.8 and publish fails the 11.5.1 requirement; `"private": true` blocks it, `files` ships `docs/` and a 2.5 MB map, no LICENSE; 40–41 rows on a 22-row budget → `ESC[2J` per keystroke; 0 in a 0×0 pty while Ink renders 80×24; no libevent/ncurses headers and CHANGES + tagged source are the primary record; it is an idle gate deferred by keystrokes; 3.7b's "end of a synchronized update again triggers a redraw" fix | 19 REJECT X1–X11 |

---

## 4. Conflicts between researchers and how they are resolved

Each conflict cites both positions. "Resolution" states which position the design should take and why; where
the tie can only be broken by a measurement or a product decision, that is said explicitly.

| # | Topic | Position A | Position B | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | Ink kitty keyboard `auto` mode | Adopt `kittyKeyboard: { mode: 'auto' }` (01 ADOPT; 03 §20 A1; 05 §15 A3; 06 ADOPT "after verifying first-frame cost"; 08 §12 A4 with flag `disambiguateEscapeCodes`; 04 §12 A5 adds `reportAlternateKeys`) | Reject `auto`: it writes `CSI ? u` before the first frame, waits a fixed 200 ms with no DA1 sentinel, and the reply leaks into `useInput` as `"[?0u"` even when detection succeeds because Ink's detector uses `'data'` while the App uses `'readable'` (07 §1.2, §11); 12 REJECT keeps kitty off in v1 for the first-frame budget; 08 §2.5 read the same code but did not observe the leak; 02 REJECT says `useInput` does not decode CSI-u at all | **B wins on the measured leak** (07 §1.2 is a real-pty probe; 08 §2.5 only read the code). v1: `mode: 'disabled'` plus the universal newline keys; v1.x: 07's owned handshake (`CSI ? u` + `CSI c`, ≥ 1 s, flag 1 only, filter replies) once the pty suite exists. 02's claim that Ink does not decode CSI-u is wrong per 06 §0 M1, 07 §1.2 item 4 and 08 §2.3 (kitty `CSI 13;2u` → `return && shift`); 02 itself lists it as open question 1. 04's `reportAlternateKeys` (flag 4) is dropped: 07 §11 and 08 §2.5 want flag 1 only and no file names a composer benefit of flag 4. |
| C2 | `incrementalRendering: true` | Adopt by default: 16× fewer bytes at 30 rows (05 §14 A, §15 A2); 7–8× fewer bytes per keystroke and zero `ESC[2K`, behind a `--render-mode` flag defaulting to on (08 §11, §12 A6) | Reject inline: Gemini enables it only in the alternate buffer, the Ink readme warns it "may cause rendering artifacts", the height budget already prevents clears (12 §5, REJECT); 08 §1.2 itself documents the desync risk (terminal wcwidth ≠ string-width or any stray write) and notes standard mode self-heals every frame; 07 §12 Q7 and 03 §22 Q10 ask for a measurement first | **Standard by default, incremental opt-in** (`--render-mode incremental`) until the soak test 08 §14 Q2 / 07 §12 Q7 describe passes on tmux, VS Code, Windows Terminal and slow links. Both sides agree the byte saving is real and the risk is unmeasured. |
| C3 | `maxFps` | 60 for the interactive TUI: the default 34 ms throttle is the worst-case keystroke latency and frames cost 0.2–0.4 ms (08 §12 A11) | Keep 30: `maxFps` above 30 or below 20 rejected (05 §16 R17); keep Ink's 30 fps cap and add `--fps` with 10 fps under SSH (12 §5, ADOPT); 15 fps under `SSH_TTY` (07 §6, §10); lower to 20 to match `LIVE_FLUSH_MS` (03 §22 Q10) | **30 by default with `--fps`/`JEVCODE_FPS`** (05, 12). 08 §1 itself shows the throttle is leading-edge (a keystroke renders immediately; only bursts wait one window), so 60 buys nothing for the first keystroke. The SSH default (10 per 12 vs 15 per 07) is an open measurement (12 OPEN Q6). |
| C4 | DEC 2026 synchronized output | Wrap each frame in `CSI ? 2026 h/l` only after a DECRQM probe reply received after the first frame, via a `Writable` proxy (02 ADOPT, §9; 02 OPEN Q2 asks where to hook it) | Ink 7.1.1 already brackets every frame in BSU/ESU unconditionally on TTYs with no query (05 §13.2; 07 §0, §2.1; 08 §1.3); live pty captures show one pair per frame (12 §0, §12.5) | **Nothing to build**; 02's proposal is already Ink behaviour (02 wrote from Claude Code's history, not from the installed Ink). Keep it and add `ESC[?2026l` to the crash-restore string (07 §2.1). |
| C5 | Undo mechanism | Shadow-git snapshots per step (`--git-dir ~/.jevcode/snapshot/<hash>`, `write-tree`/`read-tree`/`checkout-index`) (01 §2.7, §8 item 9, ADOPT); per-step workspace snapshot for a three-way rewind (02 ADOPT, OPEN Q4 on cost) | Reject shadow git: a second git tree conflicts with the seatbelt write-deny rules under `~/.jevcode` and JevCode already checkpoints each step (03 §21 R8); undo should revert a checkpointed patch (05 §16 R16); `write-tree` on 5,000 files competes with the < 50 ms budget — capture pre-images for edit/write/patch and use `git checkout --` for tracked files changed by `run` steps (10 §15.7, §17) | **B (10 §15.7), as sharpened by 15.** 01 itself flagged the cost as OPEN Q4; 10's design is O(changed files) and adopts Claude Code's honest boundary wording (02 §6.2). Claude Code's three-way menu (02 ADOPT) survives as 10's `/rewind files / plan+window / both`. Two of 10's mechanics are superseded by measurement in 15: `git checkout -- <path>` → `git restore --source=HEAD --worktree` (C40) and `git stash create` → dirty-set pre-images (C41); 15 §9 re-rejects the shadow repo with the extra cost of alternates, `feature.manyFiles` and a prune job. |
| C6 | Remembered approvals | Three-option row `Approve once / Approve for this pattern (this run) / Decline` (01 §8 item 7, ADOPT; 01 OPEN Q3 doubts it); "Yes, and don't ask again for `<kind>` `<target>` in `<workspace>`" persisted to `.jevcode/settings.local.json` skipping only the human confirm for 0.3–0.7 risk (02 ADOPT; 02 OPEN Q5); `[2] Approve this kind for the run` (03 §20 A11); `Yes, and don't ask again for <kind> this run` (04 §12 A9) | Never offer "always"/"session"/"prefix" — a remembered rule executes future actions Jev scored 0.3–0.7 without a gate, contradicting DESIGN §6 and the bench definition of reviews as declines (11 §4j, §6 A2, §7); persisted allow-lists rejected, at most an in-memory per-target approval as an open question (05 §16 R12, §17 Q3); never auto-approve (06 §12, REJECT) | **B (11 §4j).** 01 OPEN Q3, 02 OPEN Q5 and 05 OPEN Q3 all say this is a policy/Jev-side decision, not a UI choice; the UI ships once/decline/decline-with-note only. If a session-scoped rule is ever added it must go through `confirm:resolved source: session-rule` so Jev sees it (05 §17 Q3). |
| C7 | `!` shell mode | Adopt `!` from an empty prompt routed to the sandbox (01 §8 item 4, ADOPT; 02 ADOPT; 06 §17.2; 08 §8 key map, §12 A9 via `suspendTerminal`) | Not in v1: bypasses Jev's risk stage and the review flow; revisit as a human-authored `run` step whose outcome is still judged (10 §15.9, §17) | **B for v1.** The risk stage is the harness's control point (10 §17). 08's `suspendTerminal` mechanism remains the primitive for `$EDITOR`/`$PAGER` (08 §5). |
| C8 | Enter and defaults on the review prompt | Radio/list selection where Enter picks the highlighted option (03 §11, §20 A11; 04 §8, §12 A9; 01 §2.5 left/right + Enter); decline focused by default so Enter = decline unless moved (06 §12, §17.3) | No Enter-as-approve and no highlighted default; require the literal `y` (11 §4j) | **B (11), compatible with 06's intent:** Enter never approves. 06's decline-by-default focus produces the same outcome; the list-selection UIs of 03/04 are the anti-pattern 11 names. |
| C9 | Review key letters | `a` approve, `d` diff preview, `e` explain risk dimensions, `s` steer (06 §17.3); `d` = toggle diff/command preview (05 §15 A10) | `d` = decline with a note, `e` = expand preview, `w`+digit = why (11 §4j, §6 A1) | **11's set.** It is grounded in the existing confirmer and DESIGN §6: 06's `s` steer is 11's `d` note (reason fed back to the generator), 05/06's `d` diff is 11's `e` expand, 06's `e` explain is 11's `w` why. 06's extra `a` approve key is dropped: 11 wants exactly one approve key. |
| C10 | Paste placeholder threshold | ≥ 3 lines or > 150 chars (01 §2.1, ADOPT, opencode); 5 lines / 500 chars (03 §3, §20 A2, Gemini); > 1,000 chars (04 §5, §12 A6, Codex); > 10 lines (05 §15 A5, Pi); > 12 lines (06 §7, ADOPT) | > 3 lines or > 800 chars (02 §3.3, ADOPT; 07 §1.1, §10; 08 §2.4, §12 A3 — Claude Code's rule) | **> 3 lines or > 800 chars** (three files converge; 02 §3.3 adds that below 12 rows the line threshold drops). |
| C11 | Tab in the composer | Tab queues a prompt while the agent is busy (03 §4, §20 A10; 04 §5, §12 A4, A11) | Tab = completion; the composer must not register focusables so Tab keeps completion semantics (06 §6, §17.2; 08 §12 A13) | **B.** Enter already queues a steer while a run is live (10 §15.3), so Tab is free for completion. A follow-up "after the run" needs no key in JevCode: the process stays open and the next prompt is the follow-up (10 §15.4). |
| C12 | `/copy` transport | OSC 52 write with tmux passthrough as the zero-dep path; subprocess clipboard tools rejected (01 ADOPT, REJECT); OSC 52 when supported else print a path (05 §15 A19); OSC 52 write-only plus `pbcopy` fallback (07 §2.5, §10) | Native tool first (`pbcopy`/`wl-copy`/`xclip`/`xsel`), OSC 52 only behind `--osc52` (12 §9, ADOPT) | **Native tool first, OSC 52 write as fallback/opt-in** (12 order, 07 mechanics). 01's rejection targets subprocess *readers* spawned on paste, not a writer spawned on an explicit `/copy`; Terminal.app drops OSC 52 (07 §7; 12 §9). Never OSC 52 read (07 §11). |
| C13 | Homebrew | Reject a Homebrew tap: it presupposes static binaries (01 REJECT) | Adopt a tap formula in homebrew-core's Node-app shape (`std_npm_args` from the npm tarball, as `gemini-cli.rb`) (09 §3, §11.3, §12) | **B.** 01 rejected the binary-based tap opencode ships; 09 shows the Node-formula shape exists. Tap only until homebrew-core notability is verified (09 §14 Q4). |
| C14 | Terminal title | Set `JevCode \| <task>` and clear on exit (01 §2.3, ADOPT); OSC 0 title with `--no-title` (02 ADOPT); `jevcode · <task>` after sanitising (04 §12 A18) | Opt-in only (`--title`): titles are not reliably restorable (07 §2.9, §11) | **Opt-in (07) with 04's sanitisation.** All agree on clearing on exit. |
| C15 | Theme detection | `--theme auto` via an OSC 11 background query with a timeout and a dark default (02 ADOPT); one OSC 11 query after the first frame, luminance > 128 = light (03 §8, §20 A12) | No OSC 11 through Ink: the reply is shredded into three key events (07 §1.2, §2.10, §10); prefer not needing the answer — ANSI-16 named colours, no backgrounds (06 §14, REJECT; 11 §6 A9; 12 §3.3, REJECT) | **B.** `auto` is possible only if the pre-Ink stdin demux of 07 §12 Q9 is built; until then `--theme dark\|light\|daltonized\|ansi` with 02's preset names and 12's daltonized swap. |
| C16 | Leader key and palette chord | Copy opencode's leader `ctrl+x` (2000 ms) and `ctrl+p` palette (01 §2.6, §8 item 2, ADOPT) | No leader (Ctrl+X is a readline prefix; discoverability cost) and no Ctrl+P/Ctrl+K (readline history/kill-line); palette on `/` at column 0 (05 §16 R4; 06 §3, REJECT) | **B.** Keep 01's registry pattern and value grammar (`"none"\|false\|"a,b"\|[...]`), drop the leader and the chord. |
| C17 | First Ctrl+C and Esc while running | First Ctrl+C interrupts the running step as a steer via a new `Engine.interrupt()` (06 §17.1, OPEN Q3); single Esc = interrupt the step now (02 §3.8; 04 §12 A10; 05 §15 A4) | Ctrl-C keeps the existing hard `human_abort` (07 §1.6 citing DESIGN §11; 04 §6; 08 §12 A12); Esc = gentle pause at the next rule-1 commit point (`human_pause`), never a mid-stage kill (10 §15.4, §17) | **10's split:** Esc = pause at the step boundary, Esc Esc/Ctrl-C = abort, Ctrl-C twice = exit. 06's `interrupt()` maps onto 10's `engine.pause()`; the mid-stage interrupt of 02/04/05 would kill the sandbox tree and record an interrupted step (10 §17). Ctrl-C with composer text clears the draft in every file. **19 §4 shows this resolution settles only the live-run case** — the idle, review-pending and one-shot-vs-session cells were never written; see C42 and A110. |
| C18 | Esc Esc meaning | Clears the draft (saved to history) or opens the rewind menu when empty (02 §3.8, ADOPT; 06 §17.1; 07 §1.5) | Esc Esc within 2 s = abort a live run (10 §15.4) | **Resolve by state:** text in composer → clear draft to history; empty composer + live run → abort; empty composer + idle → rewind/steering menu. Both files already condition on state; 06 §17 gives the context model. |
| C19 | Prompt history persistence default | Opt-out: history file with `--no-history` / `JEVCODE_NO_HISTORY=1` (05 §15 A15; 06 ADOPT; 10 §15.5, §16) | Opt-in and redacted: "the composer's history file (if any) must be opt-in" and no keystroke history by default (12 §11, ADOPT); 03 §22 Q7 asks opt-in vs opt-out | **Resolved by 16 §4.6: on by default (opt-out).** All four agree the file must pass `config.redact` and hold prompts, not keystrokes. 16 adds the argument that closes it: the line is written by the same `createRedactor(secrets).redact` that writes `transcript.log`, *after* the composer's `addSecret` on `y`, and paste bodies never enter it (labels only), so the file cannot contain what the detector saw; an opt-in default would protect only users who know the flag — exactly not the user who pastes a key; all four surveyed tools default on. The "heuristic secret filter" this row asked for already exists as `patternRedact` (`src/core/redact.ts` `FORMAT_PATTERNS`, tested in `test/unit/config/redact.test.ts`) `[corrected per 19 §7]`; the narrower open item is which extra families to add (C44). 19 OPEN 4 records that the *default* remained formally open until 16 — this summary adopts 16's resolution (A60). |
| C20 | Width measurement | Use `string-width`/`Intl.Segmenter` (Ink's own measurer) for all width and cursor math so composer and Ink agree (07 §4, §10) | Do not import `string-width` (transitive-only; one-package rule); hand-roll the three rules and test against `string-width` for a fixture corpus (08 §8, §12 A1; 12 §7, REJECT); 03 §22 Q1 lists the options | **B**, with 07's parity requirement met by the fixture test and 08's EAW table generated at build time from the devDependency-visible `get-east-asian-width`. |
| C21 | Paste heuristics without bracketed paste | Adopt a fast-typing heuristic (≥ 3 printable events within one `readable` callback or 5 ms) (07 §1.1) | Reject Gemini's 30 ms/5 ms timers — they mis-fire on fast typists (03 §21 R1); reject Codex's 8 ms first-char hold, keep only the 120 ms Enter-is-newline window (04 §13 R5); treat a multi-char `useInput` chunk as paste-like — no timer needed because fast `ab` arrives as one call (08 §2.2, §12 A3) | **Chunk-based rule (08), no timers (03/04).** 07's "within one `readable` callback" clause is the same chunk rule; drop its 5 ms clause. |
| C22 | Take-back key for queued messages | Up from the first composer row (02 §3.9, ADOPT; 03 §4, §20 A10; 10 §15.3) | Alt+Up pops the last queued item (04 §7, §12 A11) | **Up (02/03/10).** Codex itself falls back from Alt+Up to Shift+Left under tmux, Apple Terminal, Warp and VS Code because they swallow Alt+Up (10 §5). |
| C23 | Resize debounce | 75 ms (Codex `TRANSCRIPT_REFLOW_DEBOUNCE`) (04 §12 A13) | 50 ms trailing timer (07 §3.2, §10) | **Either; 50 ms proposed.** Both are heuristics; 07's is tied to its measured 30-event storm. Decide in the design and measure with the pty resize test (12 §12.6). |
| C24 | Transcript viewer | Opt-in in-Ink overlay with vim keys (`j/k`, `C-d/C-u`, `gg/G`, `{/}`, `/`, `[` to native scrollback, `v` editor) over `transcript.log` (06 §8, §17.6, ADOPT) | Hand the terminal to `$PAGER`/`less -R` via `suspendTerminal`; an in-Ink full-height overlay hits the clear predicate (04 §12 A15; 08 §9, §13 R2) | **B.** 06's key set is what `less` already provides; the `[` write-to-scrollback is moot because `<Static>` is scrollback. 04 §14 Q1 (does suspension release modes for a pager) is answered by 08 §5's real-pty probe except for `less -R` specifically. |
| C25 | Notification default | BEL by default when the terminal reported FocusOut or focus is unknown and the event is a confirm (07 §2.7, §10) | Opt-in bell, default off (05 §15 A13); BEL only in screen-reader mode, opt-in elsewhere (12 §1.5) | **Default off; on in screen-reader mode** (05/12). When enabled, 07's per-terminal OSC table and the `9;<digit>` rule apply. Focus tracking (1004) stays off in v1 (04 §14 Q3; 01 OPEN Q8) — 07 §2.2 shows the events arrive as filterable `"[I"`/`"[O"` strings if it is enabled later. |
| C26 | Steering vs Jev semantics | A queued human message becomes a Jev directive at the next intent Choice (02 OPEN Q3; 05 §15 A9) | Directive is a `HarnessProblem { kind: 'human' }` in the plan plus the intent state's directive slot, consumed at step start after `checkBudgets()`; whether Jev's risk state should see it is open (10 §15.3, §18 Q2) | **10's consumption point is the design;** whether the directive also enters Jev's intent/risk scoring stays open (01 OPEN Q6, 02 OPEN Q3, 05 OPEN Q4, 10 OPEN Q2 are the same question). |
| C27 | `<Static>` remount | Never remount `<Static>` for any toggle (05 §16 R5) | Soft cap 20,000 items with a keyed remount that drops the array and resets `fullStaticOutput` (12 §6, ADOPT); `<Static key={sessionId}>` remount to switch transcripts (08 §12 A8) | **Compatible.** 05 rejects remounts that *replay* items; 12/08 remount with a new, small array so nothing is replayed and older rows stay in scrollback. |
| C28 | Claude Code's VS Code Shift+Enter binding payload | `sendSequence` with `text: "\x1B\r"` (ESC CR, i.e. it fakes Alt+Enter) — read from the 2.1.267 binary strings and docs (02 §3.1); 07 §1.4 cites the same value but marks its blog sources secondary | "VS Code binding sends `\u001b[13;2u`" (08 §2.5, unsourced in that sentence; `\u001b[13;2u` is what opencode's docs tell *Windows Terminal* users to send, 01 §2.1) | **02 (primary evidence).** 07's UNVERIFIED note is resolved by 02 §3.1; 08's value appears conflated with opencode's Windows Terminal advice. Design consequence: recognise both `ESC CR` and `CSI 13;2u` as newline (02 ADOPT). |
| C29 | Static append cost | ~300 B and 7 `ESC[2K` per committed line with a 6-row dynamic region at 120×40 (04 §11) | 963 B (dyn 6) / 1,506 B (budget) at 24×80; 2,163 / 3,612 B at 50×200 (08 §11) | **Not a conflict of conclusions — different content and geometry** (04: one short line, minimal composer; 08: a bordered 6-row composer with text). Both measured 0 `ESC[2J`, both reject DECSTBM/alt-screen alternatives; A107 re-runs the benchmark with the realistic ~24-row region. |
| C30 | First-frame wall numbers | 111–127 ms wall, child 80–93 ms (02 §1.6) | 80.5 ms warm / 90.1 ms cold spawn→sentinel, child 77.9 (09 §10.1); repo harness cold 89.0 / warm 80.1 (04 §11; 09 §10.1; 12 §0) | **Same child clock, different parent clocks:** 02's wall figure wraps `script` and its own timer; 09 mirrors `perf/first-frame.ts`. Use the repo harness numbers as the baseline and 02's as an upper bound. |
| C31 | chalk and `NO_COLOR` | "UNVERIFIED against the installed chalk source (not read)" (12 §3.1, UNVERIFIED) | grep of `node_modules/chalk` and `ink/build` returns zero `NO_COLOR` hits; chalk honours `FORCE_COLOR` and `--no-color` only (06 §0; 07 §0, §2.10; 11 §3.3) | **Verified by three files:** the shim is required (A83). |
| C32 | Ink kitty auto "heuristic precheck" | The installed readme says auto mode uses "a heuristic precheck (known terminals like kitty, WezTerm, Ghostty)" (quoted in 12 §7) | The code sends only `ESC[?u` with a 200 ms timeout; the readme text is stale (08 §2.5, §13 R12; 07 §1.2 measured the same) | **08/07.** The readme is wrong for 7.1.1. |
| C33 | Ctrl+Z | Not bound; document `C-z` as unsupported because raw mode swallows it (06 §17.1) | Implement: `0x1a` → restore + `SIGTSTP`/`SIGSTOP` self, `SIGCONT` → re-raw + repaint; measured working under `bash -i` + `fg` (07 §3.3, §10; 08 §5; 12 §10.1, ADOPT) | **Implement (07/08/12).** 06 wrote before the measurement existed. |
| C34 | Gemini session-browser delete key | Not read; delete key UNVERIFIED (03 §22 Q11, UNVERIFIED) | `SessionBrowser.tsx` lines 107–158: columns `Index │ Msgs │ Age │ Name`, 20 per page, `x` deletes, `/` searches, Enter, Esc (10 §3.2) | **10 resolves 03's item.** |
| C35 | `@` mention candidate source | Cached `git ls-files` respecting `.gitignore` (02 ADOPT); `fs.opendir` walk + gitignore-ish skip list (04 §12 A8) | The engine's existing candidate-file list (01 §8 item 4, ADOPT; 03 §20 A9) | **Engine list (01/03):** it already exists for the context Noul; 04's async-session/stale-result UX applies on top. |
| C36 | `/compact` | Listed in the minimum slash set with a `?` (05 §15 A7) | Rejected: the prompt is O(plan + window); `/plan`, `/decisions`, `/jev` replace it (10 §7, §15.9, §17) | **10** (repo fact: DESIGN §6 as cited by 10 §7). |
| C37 | `--continue` scope | Most recent run globally (Pi/Vibe) vs per workspace (Kilo) — open (05 §17 Q6) | Filter the index by workspace realpath by default, `Ctrl-A` widens; `--continue` = most recently *used* run in this workspace (10 §15.6; 02 ADOPT; 03 §20 A15) | **Per workspace (10/02/03).** |
| C38 | Session picker data path | Add a workspace index or scan `run.json` files at picker time (02 OPEN Q6; 04 §14 Q7 "a minimal list over `run.json` is probably enough") | Measured: index 0.13 ms vs 47 ms scan (10 §14) | **Index (10).** |
| C39 | `node:sqlite` | Stability on 22.23.2 UNVERIFIED (01 OPEN Q2, UNVERIFIED) | Loads but prints `ExperimentalWarning` (verified locally) (10 §17) | **10 resolves 01's question: reject.** |
| C40 | `/undo` recovery primitive for tracked files changed by a `run` step | `git checkout -- <path>` (10 §15.7, §16; 00 A57 as first written) | `git restore --source=HEAD --worktree -- <path>`: `git-checkout(1)` says the no-tree-ish form overwrites the worktree "with the contents in the index", and a probe showed a staged edit surviving `checkout --` and being removed only by `restore --source=HEAD` (15 §3.2, §6.4, ADOPT G7) | **15 (measured).** A `run` may have `git add`ed its own changes; the index and refs belong to the user and nothing in the harness may touch them. A57 amended. |
| C41 | Pre-images for `run` steps | `git stash create` before each `run` step as a cheap whole-tree snapshot (10 §18 Q5; 00 P9) | Copy only the currently dirty/untracked/touched set before `execute` (15 §6.3): `stash create` measured 44.8 ms p50 for 3 modified files and 84.4 ms for 53, writes 12 loose objects per 3 files into the user's `.git/objects`, excludes untracked files, fails on unborn repos; Codex's `ghost_snapshot` is "Compatibility-only … snapshots are no longer produced" (15 §2.2, §3.1, §4) | **15.** Over the 50 ms harness budget on its own; clean tracked files are recoverable from HEAD so only dirty files need a copy (O(dirty)); works in non-git workspaces for `touched`. P9 resolved. |
| C42 | Ctrl-C semantics beyond the live-run case | (i) A18 + 06 §17.1: first Ctrl-C on an empty composer = abort *and* exit 130; (iii) 06 §17.1: first Ctrl-C during a run is a *steer* (`engine.interrupt()`) | (ii) A54 + 10 §15.4: abort does not exit in session mode, second Ctrl-C exits; (iv) DESIGN §11 + today's `App.tsx:128-131`: any Ctrl-C = `shutdown('human_abort')` = exit 130 once the checkpoint is written (19 §4) | **No file enumerates the matrix; 19 §4's table is adopted as the design's starting proposal (A110, §9 D2)** with one non-negotiable: a Ctrl-C while `aborting` is set exits immediately (DESIGN §11). Open sub-questions (19 OPEN 1): abort vs steer on the first Ctrl-C of a live run in session mode; whether Ctrl-C with a review pending declines *and* aborts. 17 §4.9's exit-code table supplies the session-mode codes (`/exit` = 0 always). |
| C43 | Where a key is entered inside the TUI | Not designed; "v1 tells the user which variable to set in another shell and offers `[r]`" and asks "Does the composer ever accept a secret at all?" (17 §4.5, OPEN 1); "if a `/login`-style command ever exists, its input must be a masked field, excluded from history and trace … not designed here" (16 OPEN 4) | A full masked wizard field and `/login` re-entry that satisfy exactly 16's conditions: `useRef` buffer, no history, kind/len-only trace, `addSecret` before the Static commit, `saved — applies to the next run` (13 §5.2–5.3, §5.10) | **13 designs it; 17's 401/403 pane routes to 13's `/login` (A164 → A126) and 16's condition is met.** The composer itself never accepts a key as a *credential*: a key pasted into the composer is text subject to 16's `Send anyway? y/N` gate (A154), a key entered via `/login` is a secret that never enters the composer buffer. Two channels, one redactor. |
| C44 | Extra secret families (AWS `AKIA…`, PEM, JWT, `glpat-`, `npm_`, `xox…`) | Add them to `FORMAT_PATTERNS` (redacting) in a small PR with negative tests (19 V7) | Ship them **warn-only** in `detectSecrets` and promote to redacting only after a week of real composer prompts is re-scanned for false positives (16 §4.1, OPEN 7); 19 OPEN 3 itself notes PEM blocks and JWTs appear legitimately in coding-agent output and argues for JWT and AWS (high precision) but against a bare PEM rule without the `PRIVATE KEY` literal | **16's staging with 19's ordering:** warn-only now (0 FP on ~50 MB of agent text, but 86 of 86 local prompts are bench-authored); promote JWT and AWS first, PEM only with the `PRIVATE KEY` literal, never a generic long-token rule (the `redact.ts` header policy). Composer-confirmed values are already exact-redacted via `addSecret`, so the warn-only stage loses nothing for the human turn. |
| C45 | Scope of `/budget spend-cap <v>` | "Applies to the next run only" (10 §15.9; 00 A58) | Applies to whichever comes first — the next `/resume` of the stopped run (recorded in `run.json.overrides[]` via `reconcileResumeConfig`) or the next new run; never to a live run (14 §4.4) | **14 sharpens A58.** Both keep "a run must stay comparable with itself" (DESIGN §9); 14 adds the `/resume` route because after a `spend_cap` stop the follow-up would otherwise be the path of least resistance and silently reset the cap; the override mechanism exists and is unused (0 of 216 runs). `/budget session-spend-cap` applies immediately (A134). |
| C46 | `--no-input` semantics | Decline every review and never wait (06 §16; A101) | Onboarding-only: no wizard, print the fix block, exit 2 (13 §5.1, §5.8); 13 OPEN 7 asks whether it should also imply "decline every review" | **Both, per clig ("If `--no-input` is passed, don't prompt or do anything interactive").** One flag (`--no-input` / `JEVCODE_NO_INPUT=1`) suppresses the wizard, the trust gate, the follow-up confirm (14 §4.3 case 4 clamps silently), the secret `y/N` row (16 §4.1 cancels) and review prompts (A101); every suppressed prompt takes its safe default. |
| C47 | Height caps in the summary | Composer 1–6 (A10; 08 §12 A7); queue ≤ 2 (A53; 10 §15.3); §1 "≤ 2–3" | Composer ≤ 8 and queue ≤ 3 (A107; 04 §14 Q4; 02 §9); review box up to 16 (11 §5) — a §1 shape that sums to 40–41 rows on a 22-row budget with a review pending and 24–25 without (19 §5) | **One cap set (A109): composer 1–6 (8 only at rows ≥ 40), queue ≤ 2, pane ≤ 12, review 8 + ≤ 8, plus the new panes' caps (wizard ≤ 4, follow-up confirm 5, secret row 1, retry row inside live ≤ 2), yield order pane → live → preview → queue → composer-to-1.** 19 OPEN 2 leaves one sub-choice: whether the review box collapses the composer to one row (proposed) or hides the queue first. Q8 is the missing rule and is decision D1 in §9. |
| C48 | Review-notification timer | "Fire on a pending review after ~6 s idle" read as a delay from the request (A85; 02 §5.2, §7.3) | Claude Code's docs: "once you haven't typed for about six seconds. The timer starts when the permission prompt appears, and each keystroke defers it" — an idle gate with debounce; a fixed delay only in SDK-hosted sessions (19 §12) | **19's semantics.** Start the timer when the *deferred* review box appears (A41's ~1 s idle rule), restart on every keystroke, fire at ~6 s; run-end timer starts at `run:end`, cancelled by typing, fires at ~60 s. 19 OPEN 9 asks whether the timer should instead start at the *request*; V12's "at the box" is adopted because it matches the quoted text. |
| C49 | Trust-decision file location | Config under XDG (`~/.config/jevcode`, 09 §7; A73) | `~/.jevcode/trust.json` next to runs, inside the seatbelt-protected tree (13 §5.7); 13 OPEN 8 notes the XDG dir is readable by sandboxed commands unless added to `secretPaths` | **Open (P40); 13's proposal is the default.** The deciding fact is that `~/.jevcode` is already denied to sandboxed commands while a new XDG file would need a `secretPaths` entry; portability argues for XDG. Decide with D6 in §9. |
| C50 | Paste size vs what the generator sees | 1 MiB paste cap for parity with `MAX_CANDIDATE_BYTES` (16 §4.3) | `PROMPT_LIMITS.taskChars = 12_000` clips the human turn in `src/provider/prompts.ts:19, 247`, so a long paste is silently truncated for the generator while Jev state has its own bounds (16 OPEN 9) | **Open (P41).** Either lower the paste cap to match, or (16's alternative) have the composer say `only the first 12,000 characters reach the generator; @-mention a file for more` when a submission exceeds it. The second keeps the paste useful for `@`-style recall and is the proposal. |
| C51 | Budget warning thresholds | 06 §11: "spend > 80 %" as the one `<Static>`-worthy threshold; Claude Code gateway warns at 75 % and 95 % (14 §3.1) | 50 / 80 / 95 % per scope (14 §4.2) | **14.** 50 % is the earliest point at which "about N steps left" is a usable projection (p90 cost/step is 26 % of a p50 run), 80 % is 06's number, 95 % matches the gateway's second warning; 34/4/1 of 186 live runs would have fired — frequent enough to inform, rare enough not to be noise. |

---

## 5. Every measured number

"Measured" = obtained by a researcher's own benchmark, probe, count or registry read. Values are quoted as the
files state them (rounded where the file rounds). §5.8 lists design constants that were *read* from reference
tools, not measured; §5.9 lists digest-vs-file discrepancies.

### 5.3 Input parsing and terminal behaviour

| What | Value | How | Source |

Notes: (a) Terminal.app has no primary Apple source for any protocol row (07 §12 Q1; 12 OPEN Q2). (b) mosh: only
bracketed paste (2013), mouse (1.2.5) and truecolor (1.4.0) were sourced; OSC 52/8, 1004, kitty and 2026 are
treated as unsupported (07 §6). (c) Inside tmux `COLORTERM` from the outer terminal is not visible, so use 16
named colours (07 §6). (d) Ghostty's `clipboard-paste-protection` confirmation is skipped for bracketed pastes
(`clipboard-paste-bracketed-safe` default true), so enabling 2004 also removes a dialog (07 §1.1). (e) can-i-use-terminal's
version fields are unreliable — its VS Code OSC 52 version was two releases late — so the kitty ≥ 0.36.2, Windows
Terminal 1.2.2022.0 and tmux 2.5 figures in the OSC 52 row of §5.7, which came from it, are unaudited (19 §8, OPEN 10;
U47). (f) Never gate a feature on a version string: VS Code's kitty default was `false` behind a server-side experiment
at 1.109 and `true` only from 1.110; detect at runtime with DA1 + the `CSI ? u` reply (19 §9, X2; A111). (g) Inside tmux
any outstanding query raises `escape-time` to a 500 ms floor, delaying a typed Esc by up to that much (19 §11; A112).

Each item names every file that raised it. Items marked **resolved by** were answered by another file's
measurement or reading; they stay listed so the trail is visible.

## 8. Coverage after files 13–17: what a design document would still need

Files 01–12 cover the reference tools, Ink internals, terminal protocols, sessions, the Jev-native views,
packaging, accessibility and testing; files 13–17 were written to close gaps 7, 9, 10 and 11 below and touch 2, 4,
5, 6, 12, 14, 15 and 20 on the way. The original 24-item list is kept verbatim for traceability (each "touched in"
pointer is the closest material in 01–12); the **status table after it** records, per item, what 13–17 and 19 now
cover and what remains, followed by the residual gaps those files themselves opened.

1. **Composer ↔ engine wiring.** The exact `Engine` API additions (`steer`, `unsteer`, `pause`, `seed`) are
   proposed (10 §15.2–15.4; 06 OPEN 3) but no file specifies the React/`useEngine` state shape for the composer
   (draft, queue, history level, palette state), how composer state coexists with the existing `uiReducer`, or
   how `--plain`'s readline composer shares the slash-command dispatcher (12 §11 mentions it only).
2. **Height-budget allocation rule with a composer.** Every file asks (01 OPEN 5; 02 OPEN 8; 03 OPEN 4; 05 OPEN 1;
   06 OPEN 4) and 11 §5 gives arithmetic for the review box, but no file states the final `computeLayout()`
   order including composer, queue preview, suggestion list, loop banner and tabbed pane at rows 8/12/24/40.
3. **Slash-command grammar.** Argument parsing, quoting, argument completion (`completion(partial)` is named in
   03 §20 A8), error reporting for bad arguments, and the `availableDuringTask` table per command are not
   specified; 10 §15.9 gives one-line semantics only.
4. **The `--json` event schema.** 05 §15 A18 and 10 §15.10 name the stream and four new events but no file
   defines field-level schema, versioning or redaction guarantees for scripts.
5. **A consolidated `ui.*` config schema.** Settings appear piecemeal (`ui.screenReader`, `ui.reducedMotion`,
   `ui.osc52`, `updateNotify`, `--theme`, `--fps`, `--render-mode`, `--ascii`, `--title`, `--no-history`,
   keybindings file) across 02, 05, 07, 09, 11, 12; precedence (flag > env > file) is stated only for
   screen-reader and colour (12 §1.5, §3.1).
6. **`run.json`/index schema migration.** 10 §15.1 adds `sessionId`, `parentRunId`, `source`, `instructions[]`,
   `planAfter` and classifies legacy runs by path prefix, but compatibility with existing `--resume` readers, the
   checkpoint `version` field and bench readers is not worked through.
7. **Diff rendering inside Ink.** 04 §9 records Codex's palette and `/diff` mechanics and 01 §2.2 opencode's
   viewer; the JevCode confirm preview and `/diff` output format (unified vs stat, colour-blind-safe signs,
   truncation) are not designed beyond "through `$PAGER`" (04 §12 A15; 08 §9).
8. **Markdown/prose rendering of generator text.** 03 §21 R7 rejects full highlighting and suggests fences and
   headings only on the committed proposal item; no file designs that renderer or measures its cost.
9. **Provider/network error UX in the TUI.** Rate limits, retries, provider outages and spend-cap stops are
   engine events; no file designs how they appear (toast vs Static item vs status zone) beyond 06 §11's toast
   rule and 10 §15.9's `/budget`.
10. **Home screen / first-run experience.** `jevcode` with no task opening a composer is proposed (01 §8 item 8;
    10 §15.4) and Claude Code's onboarding theme picker is observed (02 §1.6), but the JevCode first-run flow
    (missing API key, config wizard, workspace trust, placeholder text) is not designed.
11. **Interactive secret handling.** Redaction of history and notes is covered (10 §15.5; 11 §4j); entering or
    rotating provider keys from the TUI, and a secret-file denylist for `@` mentions (06 §17.2 mentions one) are
    not.
12. **Workspace switching inside a session** (`/cd`-style; Codex has `Cd`, 04 §9) and multi-workspace pickers
    are not addressed beyond `Ctrl-A` widening the picker (10 §15.6).
13. **`run.lock` and concurrency semantics** are named (10 §17) but not specified (stale-lock detection, PID
    liveness, what a second TUI shows).
14. **AGENTS.md prompt-injection review.** 10 §15.8 injects project instructions into the generator system
    prompt; no file analyses the trust model or interaction with the redactor and the risk stage.
15. **Real-terminal performance of the composer.** All keystroke/frame numbers are from fake TTY streams (08
    §11, UNVERIFIED note) or a 6-row region (04 §11); no file measured composer keystroke → frame latency in a
    real pty at the realistic dynamic-region size (A107 is the placeholder).
16. **Windows and ConPTY.** No measurement possible (05 OPEN 9; 09 OPEN 6); the Windows console full-clear rule
    (08 §1) and ConPTY paste quirks (04 §5) are recorded but no test plan exists.
17. **Screen-reader user validation.** 12 §1.5 designs the mode from Codex/Claude Code evidence; no file has a
    plan for testing with NVDA/VoiceOver users (12 OPEN 1, 4).
18. **Help overlay and KEYS.md content.** 06 §17.5 sketches the overlay; the generated docs format, per-terminal
    notes and the sync test are not specified.
19. **Undo of steering, and steering while a review is pending.** 10 §15.3 excludes `y`/`n` from steering and
    provides `unsteer`; interactions such as steering text typed while a confirm box is open, or `/undo` after a
    steer was applied, are not covered.
20. **Bench and perf integration of the new frames.** 12 §12.5 extends the gates; no file states which new
    frames (composer, palette, picker, review with gauges) join `perf/first-frame.ts` and `perf/render-lag.ts`,
    nor how the bench's `alwaysDecline` confirmer maps onto the new review keys (05 §16 R15 mentions it).
21. **Keyboard layouts and dead keys.** 07 §1.7 notes dead keys and Option-as-Meta; non-US layouts, AltGr and
    `Ctrl+/` variants (06 OPEN 5) have no design.
22. **Fuzzy matcher specification and performance.** A dependency-free subsequence scorer is assumed by 03 §20
    A8, 04 §12 A8 and 06 §3; its algorithm, ranking (frecency? 01 §2.1) and the 16 ms/keystroke budget at 5,000
    files (01 OPEN 7) are unspecified and unmeasured.
23. **Documentation deliverables.** The man page, `docs/KEYS.md`, the terminal-matrix checklist file
    (`docs/research/tui/terminal-matrix.md`, 07 §8) and a `/terminal-setup`-style per-terminal guide (02 §3.1;
    05 §15 A3) are named but their content is not.
24. **Localisation** is explicitly deferred (12 §8); RTL/bidi is punted to logical order (07 §4).

### 8.2 Residual gaps opened by files 13–17 and 19

25. **The Ctrl-C / Esc / Ctrl-D matrix across {idle, text, live, review} × {one-shot, session}** — four incompatible
    readings coexist and only the live-run cell was resolved (19 §4; §4 C42; A110; P67).
26. **A worked layout at rows 12 and 40 with the new panes** (wizard, follow-up confirm, secret row, retry row, git zone)
    under the A109 cap set — 19 §5 gives only the 24-row example (P68).
27. **Engine contract additions as one list**: `Engine.steer/unsteer/pause`, `budget:warn/stop/clamp/override/unpriced`,
    `retry`, `retry:settled`, `notice`, `workspace`, extended `run:ready`, extended `SerializedError`,
    `SpendSnapshot.parentExceeded`, `AskOptions.onRetry`/`GenerateOptions.onRetry`, `config.missingSecrets`,
    `Redactor.dropSecret`, `workspace.readSecretForMention`, `GitState`/`statusPorcelainV2`, `ProfileOptions.gitDir/gitCommonDir`,
    `StopReason 'token_cap'` — each is specified in its file (10, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17) but nowhere collected or ordered.
28. **Exit-code changes that touch DESIGN §11**: first-call 401/403 and first-call drift → 2 (today 5), degraded-checkpoint
    stop → 3 (today 0), `/exit` → 0 always, session-mode `run:end` items carrying the code (17 §4.9; P62).
29. **Seatbelt profile for linked worktrees, subdirectory workspaces and submodules** (15 §5; A149) — a sandbox change, not a
    TUI one, but the harness's own `git restore` fails without it.
30. **Trust-decision persistence and the `AGENTS.md` sha256 drift rule** (13 §5.7; C49; P38–P39).
31. **Composer-added secret scope across `--resume`** (16 OPEN 2; P56) and the paste-cap vs prompt-clip mismatch (C50; P44).
32. **The unpriced-model policy's blast radius**: refusing to start for any Anthropic model outside the three-entry table
    changes the CLI's default behaviour for every unlisted model id; the design must say how the table is extended and
    whether `--allow-unpriced` is discoverable in the refusal message (14 §4.5; A135).
33. **Where new-pane performance is measured**: all wizard/confirm/secret-row/retry-row render numbers are from fake TTY
    streams or a scratch bundle (Q41; U43–U44).
34. **A release checklist that actually publishes**: `"private": true`, `LICENSE`, `files`, npm ≥ 11.5.1 on the publish
    job (19 §6, §10; A65, A72; P69).

---

## 9. Ready for design: the 15 decisions the design document must make

Each decision names the research that informs it (file and section) and the summary rows that already fix parts of
it. Every decision inherits the standing rules: `<Static>` is the only scrollback writer, the dynamic region stays
within `rows − 2`, zero network before the first frame, zero terminal clears after it, rendering never blocks the
loop, keys never appear in logs, and every visual surface ships `--plain`, `--screen-reader` and `--ascii` twins that
agree line for line with `transcript.log` (A25, A49, A80, A90, A94–A98).

| # | Decision | What must be fixed | Informing research |

| D1 | **Layout cap set and yield order** for the dynamic region, with worked frames at rows 8/12/24/40 | Composer 1–6 (8 at ≥ 40 rows), queue ≤ 2, pane ≤ 12, review 8 + ≤ 8, wizard ≤ 4, follow-up confirm 5, secret row 1, retry row inside live ≤ 2; yield pane → live → preview → queue → composer-to-1; whether the review box collapses the composer or hides the queue; `computeLayout()` order | 08 §11, §12 A7 (bytes per keystroke, overflow cost); 04 §14 Q4; 10 §15.3; 11 §5 (review arithmetic); 12 §4 (min 40×8); 13 §5.12; 14 §4.3, §5.3; 16 §3.3; 17 §4.1; 19 §5, V5 — A10, A107, A109; C47; Q8, P68 |
| D2 | **Key and exit semantics**: the Ctrl-C / Esc / Ctrl-D matrix over {idle-empty, idle-text, live-empty, live-text, review pending} × {one-shot, session}, the double-press window, and the session-mode exit-code table | Which cells abort vs steer vs clear vs exit; the 1/1.5/3 s window; `/exit` = 0 always; first-call 401/403 → 2; degraded checkpoint → 3; DESIGN §11's "second Ctrl-C while aborting exits immediately" kept | 06 §1, §12, §17.1; 07 §1.5, §1.6; 10 §15.4; 11 §4j; 02 §3.8; 17 §4.9; 19 §4, V4 — A18–A20, A54, A110; C17, C18, C42; P25, P62, P67 |
